Excellent! Now I have a complete picture. Let me compile the comprehensive findings for the user.

## Load Testing Files - Complete Summary

I've found and analyzed the load testing infrastructure in this project. Here's everything you need to know:

### 1. **Location of Load Test Scripts**

All load tests are located in `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/tests/load/`:
- **Scenarios:** `src/scenarios/` (5 test files)
- **Workloads:** `src/workloads/` (3 workload implementations)
- **Harness:** `src/harness/` (client pool, metrics collection, reporting)

### 2. **Framework & Tool**

**Framework:** Node.js-based (NOT k6 or artillery)
- Test runner: **Vitest 3.0.5**
- Each "virtual user" is a real `CipherBoxClient` instance
- Uses `Promise.allSettled()` for concurrent operations
- All packages: TypeScript/ESM with Web Crypto API

**Why Node.js instead of k6:**
- k6 uses a Go-based JS runtime that lacks support for full Node.js crypto ecosystem (`@noble/*`, `eciesjs`, Web Crypto API)
- The SDK packages are pure TypeScript/ESM
- Network I/O is the bottleneck, not CPU

### 3. **5 Load Test Scenarios**

| Scenario | Default Clients | Focus | Command |
|----------|-----------------|-------|---------|
| **upload-throughput** | 10 × 20 files | Upload pipeline + IPNS publish | `test:single -- upload-throughput` |
| **ipns-publish-storm** | 20 × 50 cycles | IPNS publish contention (system bottleneck) | `test:single -- ipns-publish-storm` |
| **mixed-workload** | 5 × 45 ops | Realistic weighted operation mix (createFolder 2, uploadFile 4, rename/move/delete 1 each) | `test:single -- mixed-workload` |
| **sustained-load** | 5 × 5 min (configurable) | Latency stability over time (detects memory leaks, connection pool exhaustion) | `test:single -- sustained-load` |
| **spike-test** | 2→20 burst | Recovery time measurement (baseline phase → burst phase) | `test:single -- spike-test` |

### 4. **Configuration Options**

**Environment Variables** (in `.env` or set before running):

```bash
# Target API URL
LOAD_TEST_API_URL=http://localhost:3000

# Secret for /auth/test-login
LOAD_TEST_SECRET=[REDACTED]

# Number of concurrent virtual users (CRITICAL for controlling clients)
LOAD_TEST_CLIENTS=5

# Test duration in seconds for sustained scenarios
LOAD_TEST_DURATION=300

# Throttle bypass secret (must match THROTTLE_BYPASS_SECRET in API .env)
THROTTLE_BYPASS_SECRET=[REDACTED]
```

**Example template:** `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/tests/load/.env.example`

### 5. **How to Run Load Tests**

#### **Locally:**
```bash
# Prerequisites: PostgreSQL, IPFS (Kubo), Redis on host
# API .env must have NODE_ENV=test

# Start mock IPNS routing
node tools/mock-ipns-routing/dist/index.js &

# Start API
pnpm --filter @cipherbox/api dev &

# Run a specific scenario with custom client count
LOAD_TEST_CLIENTS=5 pnpm --filter @cipherbox/load-tests test:single -- mixed-workload
LOAD_TEST_CLIENTS=20 pnpm --filter @cipherbox/load-tests test:single -- ipns-publish-storm
```

#### **Against Staging (https://api-staging.cipherbox.cc):**
Use GitHub Actions workflow dispatch:
- **File:** `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/.github/workflows/load-test.yml`
- **Inputs:**
  - Environment: `staging` or `local`
  - Client count: any positive integer (default 5)
  - Scenario: one of the 5 above
  
GitHub Actions will:
1. Automatically configure `LOAD_TEST_API_URL` to `https://api-staging.cipherbox.cc`
2. Inject staging credentials (`STAGING_TEST_LOGIN_SECRET`, `STAGING_THROTTLE_BYPASS_SECRET`) from GitHub environment secrets
3. Run the scenario
4. Upload metrics JSON to artifacts (retention: 30 days)

### 6. **Rate Limiting & Throttle Bypass**

The API has built-in rate limiting (10 req/s, 100 req/min). For testing:

| Environment | Mechanism | Throttle Behavior |
|-------------|-----------|-------------------|
| **Local/CI** | `NODE_ENV=test` | Limits relaxed to 200 req/s, 10k req/min |
| **Staging** | `X-Throttle-Bypass` header + `THROTTLE_BYPASS_SECRET` | Bypassed for throughput tests; real limits for throttle validation |
| **Production** | No bypass secret configured | Real limits always enforced |

The bypass is transparent to tests — client pool automatically includes the header when `THROTTLE_BYPASS_SECRET` is set.

### 7. **Metrics & Reporting**

Each load test:
- Collects **per-operation latency percentiles** (p50, p95, p99, min, max, avg)
- Measures **throughput** (ops/sec)
- Tracks **errors** and **bytes transferred**
- Prints console table summary
- Writes **JSON metrics file** for CI artifact upload: `metrics-{scenario-slug}.json`

**Metrics structure:**
```json
{
  "scenario": "Mixed Workload",
  "clientCount": 5,
  "totalDurationMs": 12345,
  "totalOps": 225,
  "totalErrors": 3,
  "operations": [
    {
      "operation": "createFolder",
      "count": 45,
      "errors": 1,
      "latency": { "p50": 234, "p95": 567, "p99": 890, "max": 1200, "min": 150, "avg": 300 },
      "throughputOpsPerSec": 3.6,
      "bytesTransferred": null
    },
    // ... more operations
  ],
  "timestamp": "2026-03-23T15:04:00.000Z"
}
```

### 8. **Client Pool Architecture**

- **Parallel account creation:** Creates test accounts in batches of 5 (to avoid overwhelming API)
- **Instance-scoped axios:** Each client has its own axios instance → no shared singleton → true parallel operation
- **Metrics per client:** Each virtual user collects independent metrics, then aggregates
- **Cleanup:** Automatically deletes test accounts and zeros sensitive key material after test

### 9. **Directory Structure**

```
tests/load/
├── .env.example                           # Config template
├── .gitignore
├── package.json                           # Vitest, @cipherbox/* packages
├── tsconfig.json
├── vitest.config.ts                       # 10min timeout, sequential tests
├── src/
│   ├── harness/
│   │   ├── client-pool.ts                 # createClientPool, destroyClientPool, aggregateAndReport
│   │   ├── metrics.ts                     # MetricsCollector, OperationMetrics, OperationSample
│   │   └── reporter.ts                    # printSummary, toJsonReport
│   ├── scenarios/                         # 5 test files
│   │   ├── upload-throughput.test.ts
│   │   ├── ipns-publish-storm.test.ts
│   │   ├── mixed-workload.test.ts
│   │   ├── sustained-load.test.ts
│   │   └── spike-test.test.ts
│   └── workloads/                         # Reusable operation sequences
│       ├── folder-workload.ts             # createFolder, renameItem, deleteItem cycles
│       ├── file-workload.ts               # uploadFile with variable sizes
│       └── mixed-workload.ts              # Weighted mix of all operations
├── metrics-*.json                         # Generated reports (gitignored)
└── node_modules/
```

### 10. **Key Configuration Files**

- **GitHub Actions:** `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/.github/workflows/load-test.yml` (workflow_dispatch, environment/client_count/scenario inputs)
- **Test config:** `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/tests/load/vitest.config.ts` (600s timeout, sequential execution, no file parallelism)
- **Testing strategy doc:** `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/tests/TESTING_STRATEGY.md` (comprehensive rate limiting & architecture details)

### To Point Tests at Staging

For **manual runs** (requires environment setup):
```bash
LOAD_TEST_API_URL=https://api-staging.cipherbox.cc \
LOAD_TEST_SECRET=<staging-secret> \
LOAD_TEST_CLIENTS=10 \
THROTTLE_BYPASS_SECRET=<staging-bypass-secret> \
pnpm --filter @cipherbox/load-tests test:single -- mixed-workload
```

For **CI runs** (recommended):
1. Navigate to GitHub Actions → Load Tests workflow
2. Click "Run workflow"
3. Select environment: `staging`
4. Enter client count (e.g., 20)
5. Select scenario (e.g., `ipns-publish-storm`)
6. Metrics auto-download from artifacts

> AGENT

Two issues:

1. **`--testPathPattern` is a Jest option, not Vitest** — Vitest uses positional args for file filtering
2. **Missing `environment:` on the job** — staging secrets/vars are scoped to the `staging` GitHub environment, but the job doesn't declare it, so all env vars are empty

Let me fix both:

> AGENT

PR created: https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/pull/337

Two bugs found:

1. **`--testPathPattern` is Jest, not Vitest** — the `test:single` script was using a Jest CLI flag that Vitest doesn't recognize. Fixed by removing it (Vitest accepts file filter patterns as positional args).

2. **Missing `environment: staging` on the job** — the staging secrets (`STAGING_TEST_LOGIN_SECRET`, `STAGING_THROTTLE_BYPASS_SECRET`) and vars (`STAGING_API_URL`) are scoped to the `staging` GitHub environment, but the job never declared it. That's why all three env vars were empty in the logs.

Once you merge the PR, I can re-trigger the 10/20/30 client runs.

   203→      - name: Check for entity/migration drift
   204→        working-directory: apps/api
   205→        run: |
   206→          set +e
   207→          OUTPUT="$(pnpm typeorm migration:generate src/migrations/DriftCheck -d src/data-source.ts 2>&1)"
   208→          STATUS=$?
   209→          set -e
   210→
   211→          # Check if migration:generate failed before inspecting output files
   212→          if [ "$STATUS" -ne 0 ]; then
   213→            if echo "$OUTPUT" | grep -q "No changes in database schema were found"; then
   214→              echo "No entity/migration drift detected."
   215→              exit 0
   216→            fi
   217→            echo "::error::migration:generate failed unexpectedly."
   218→            echo "$OUTPUT"
   219→            exit 1
   220→          fi
   221→
   222→          if ls src/migrations/*DriftCheck*.ts 1>/dev/null 2>&1; then
   223→            # Our hand-written migrations use explicit constraint/index names, but TypeORM
   224→            # expects auto-generated hash-based names. Filter out cosmetic renames and only
   225→            # fail on real structural drift (new/dropped tables, added/removed columns, type changes).
   226→            if grep -qE 'CREATE TABLE|DROP TABLE|ADD COLUMN|DROP COLUMN|ALTER.*TYPE|RENAME COLUMN' src/migrations/*DriftCheck*.ts; then
   227→              echo "::error::Entity/migration drift detected! Entities define schema changes not covered by migrations."
   228→              echo ""
   229→              echo "Generated migration contents:"
   230→              cat src/migrations/*DriftCheck*.ts
   231→              echo ""
   232→              echo "Fix: Run 'pnpm --filter @cipherbox/api typeorm migration:generate src/migrations/<Name> -d src/data-source.ts' and commit the result."
   233→              exit 1
   234→            else
   235→              echo "Only cosmetic constraint/index naming differences detected (not structural drift). OK."
   236→            fi
   237→          else
   238→            echo "No entity/migration drift detected."
   239→          fi
   240→        env:
   241→          DB_HOST: localhost
   242→          DB_PORT: 5432
   243→          DB_USERNAME: postgres
   244→          DB_PASSWORD: postgres
   245→          DB_DATABASE: cipherbox_test

   350→  sdk-e2e:
   351→    name: SDK E2E Tests
   352→    needs: [changes, lint]
   353→    if: needs.changes.outputs.src == 'true'
   354→    runs-on: ubuntu-latest

   356→
   357→    services:
   358→      postgres:
   359→        image: postgres:16-alpine

   361→          POSTGRES_USER: postgres
   362→          POSTGRES_PASSWORD: postgres
   363→          POSTGRES_DB: cipherbox_test
   364→        ports:
   365→          - 5432:5432
   366→        options: >-
   367→          --health-cmd pg_isready
   368→          --health-interval 10s
   369→          --health-timeout 5s
   370→          --health-retries 5
   371→
   372→      ipfs:
   373→        image: ipfs/kubo:v0.40.0
   374→        ports:
   375→          - 5001:5001
   376→          - 8080:8080
   377→        options: >-
   378→          --health-cmd "ipfs id"
   379→          --health-interval 10s
   380→          --health-timeout 5s
   381→          --health-retries 10
   382→          --health-start-period 30s
   383→

   386→        ports:
   387→          - 6379:6379
   388→        options: >-
   389→          --health-cmd "redis-cli ping"
   390→          --health-interval 10s
   391→          --health-timeout 5s
   392→          --health-retries 5
   393→
   394→    steps:
   395→      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
   396→
   397→      - uses: pnpm/action-setup@v4
   398→        with:
   399→          version: 10
   400→
   401→      - uses: actions/setup-node@v4
   402→        with:
   403→          node-version: '22'
   404→          cache: 'pnpm'
   405→
   406→      - name: Install dependencies
   407→        run: pnpm install --frozen-lockfile
   408→
   409→      - name: Install mock-ipns-routing
   410→        run: |
   411→          cd tools/mock-ipns-routing
   412→          npm install
   413→          npm run build
   414→
   415→      - name: Build packages
   416→        run: |
   417→          pnpm --filter @cipherbox/crypto build
   418→          pnpm --filter @cipherbox/core build
   419→          pnpm --filter @cipherbox/api-client build
   420→          pnpm --filter @cipherbox/sdk-core build
   421→          pnpm --filter @cipherbox/sdk build
   422→          pnpm --filter @cipherbox/api build
   423→
   424→      - name: Create API .env
   425→        run: |
   426→          echo "NODE_ENV=test" > apps/api/.env
   427→          echo "DB_HOST=localhost" >> apps/api/.env
   428→          echo "DB_PORT=5432" >> apps/api/.env
   429→          echo "DB_USERNAME=postgres" >> apps/api/.env
   430→          echo "DB_PASSWORD=postgres" >> apps/api/.env
   431→          echo "DB_DATABASE=cipherbox_test" >> apps/api/.env
   432→          echo "JWT_SECRET=[REDACTED]" >> apps/api/.env
   433→          echo "CORS_ALLOWED_ORIGINS=http://localhost:5173" >> apps/api/.env
   434→          echo "IPFS_PROVIDER=local" >> apps/api/.env
   435→          echo "IPFS_LOCAL_API_URL=http://localhost:5001" >> apps/api/.env
   436→          echo "IPFS_LOCAL_GATEWAY_URL=http://localhost:8080" >> apps/api/.env
   437→          echo "DELEGATED_ROUTING_URL=http://localhost:3001" >> apps/api/.env
   438→          echo "REDIS_HOST=localhost" >> apps/api/.env
   439→          echo "REDIS_PORT=6379" >> apps/api/.env

   452→          pnpm --filter @cipherbox/api start:prod &

   454→            if curl -fsS http://localhost:3000/health > /dev/null 2>&1; then

   457→            fi
   458→            sleep 1

   461→            echo "API failed to start within 30s"
   462→            exit 1
   463→          fi
   464→        env:

   467→          DB_PORT: 5432

   469→          DB_PASSWORD: postgres

   475→          IPFS_LOCAL_GATEWAY_URL: http://localhost:8080

   477→          REDIS_HOST: localhost
   478→          REDIS_PORT: 6379
   479→          TEST_LOGIN_SECRET=[REDACTED]

   486→          THROTTLE_BYPASS_SECRET=[REDACTED]

   488→  build:
   489→    name: Build
   490→    needs: [changes, lint]
   491→    if: needs.changes.outputs.src == 'true'
   492→    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
   493→    steps:
   494→      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
   495→
   496→      - uses: pnpm/action-setup@v4
   497→        with:
   498→          version: 10
   499→
   500→      - uses: actions/setup-node@v4
   501→        with:
   502→          node-version: '22'
   503→          cache: 'pnpm'
   504→
   505→      - name: Install dependencies
   506→        run: pnpm install --frozen-lockfile
   507→
   508→      - name: Build packages (excluding desktop)
   509→        run: |
   510→          pnpm --filter @cipherbox/crypto build
   511→          pnpm --filter @cipherbox/core build
   512→          pnpm --filter @cipherbox/api-client build
   513→          pnpm --filter @cipherbox/sdk-core build
   514→          pnpm --filter @cipherbox/sdk build
   515→          pnpm --filter @cipherbox/api build
   516→          pnpm --filter @cipherbox/web build
   517→
   518→  cargo-windows:
   519→    name: Cargo Check & Test (Windows)
   520→    needs: [changes, lint]
   521→    if: |
   522→      !failure() && !cancelled() &&
   523→      needs.changes.outputs.desktop == 'true'
   524→    runs-on: windows-latest
   525→    steps:
   526→      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
   527→
   528→      - name: Install WinFsp
   529→        shell: powershell
   530→        run: |
   531→          $url = "https://github.com/winfsp/winfsp/releases/download/v2.1/winfsp-2.1.25156.msi"
   532→          $out = "winfsp.msi"
   533→          $expectedHash = "073A70E00F77423E34BED98B86E600DEF93393BA5822204FAC57A29324DB9F7A"
   534→          Invoke-WebRequest -Uri $url -OutFile $out
   535→          $actualHash = (Get-FileHash $out -Algorithm SHA256).Hash
   536→          if ($actualHash -ne $expectedHash) { throw "WinFsp MSI hash mismatch: expected $expectedHash, got $actualHash" }
   537→          Start-Process msiexec.exe -ArgumentList "/i","$out","/qn","INSTALLLEVEL=1000" -Wait -NoNewWindow
   538→
   539→      - name: Create WinFsp MSI placeholder for resource glob
   540→        shell: powershell
   541→        run: New-Item -ItemType File -Force -Path "apps/desktop/src-tauri/resources/winfsp-placeholder.msi"
   542→
   543→      - name: Install Rust toolchain
   544→        run: rustup default stable
   545→
   546→      - uses: actions/cache@v4
   547→        with:
   548→          path: |
   549→            ~/.cargo/registry
   550→            ~/.cargo/git
   551→            apps/desktop/src-tauri/target
   552→          key: windows-cargo-${{ hashFiles('apps/desktop/src-tauri/Cargo.lock') }}
   553→          restore-keys: windows-cargo-
   554→
   555→      - name: Cargo check
   556→        run: cargo check --manifest-path apps/desktop/src-tauri/Cargo.toml --no-default-features --features winfsp
   557→
   558→      - name: Cargo test
   559→        run: cargo test --manifest-path apps/desktop/src-tauri/Cargo.toml --no-default-features --features winfsp
   560→
   561→  cargo-macos:
   562→    name: Cargo Check & Test (macOS)
   563→    needs: [changes, lint]
   564→    if: |
   565→      !failure() && !cancelled() &&
   566→      needs.changes.outputs.desktop == 'true'
   567→    runs-on: macos-latest
   568→    steps:
   569→      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
   570→
   571→      - name: Install FUSE-T
   572→        run: |
   573→          brew install --cask fuse-t
   574→          sudo mkdir -p /usr/local/lib/pkgconfig
   575→          FUSE_T_PC="/Library/Application Support/fuse-t/pkgconfig/fuse-t.pc"
   576→          if [ ! -f "$FUSE_T_PC" ]; then
   577→            FUSE_T_PC=$(find /Library /usr/local /opt/homebrew -name "fuse-t.pc" 2>/dev/null | head -1)
   578→          fi
   579→          if [ -z "$FUSE_T_PC" ] || [ ! -f "$FUSE_T_PC" ]; then
   580→            echo "::error::fuse-t.pc not found after FUSE-T installation"
   581→            exit 1
   582→          fi
   583→          sudo cp "$FUSE_T_PC" /usr/local/lib/pkgconfig/fuse.pc
   584→          sudo sed -i '' 's/^Version:.*/Version: 2.9.9/' /usr/local/lib/pkgconfig/fuse.pc
   585→
   586→      - name: Install Rust toolchain
   587→        run: rustup default stable
   588→
   589→      - uses: actions/cache@v4
   590→        with:
   591→          path: |
   592→            ~/.cargo/registry
   593→            ~/.cargo/git
   594→            apps/desktop/src-tauri/target
   595→          key: macos-cargo-${{ hashFiles('apps/desktop/src-tauri/Cargo.lock') }}
   596→          restore-keys: macos-cargo-
   597→
   598→      - name: Cargo check
   599→        working-directory: apps/desktop/src-tauri
   600→        run: cargo check --no-default-features --features fuse
   601→        env:
   602→          PKG_CONFIG_PATH: /usr/local/lib/pkgconfig
   603→
   604→      - name: Cargo test
   605→        run: cargo test --manifest-path apps/desktop/src-tauri/Cargo.toml --no-default-features --features fuse
   606→        env:
   607→          PKG_CONFIG_PATH: /usr/local/lib/pkgconfig
   608→
   609→  cargo-linux:
   610→    name: Cargo Check, Test & Coverage (Linux)
   611→    needs: [changes, lint]
   612→    if: |
   613→      !failure() && !cancelled() &&
   614→      needs.changes.outputs.desktop == 'true'
   615→    runs-on: ubuntu-22.04
   616→    steps:

   618→
   619→      - name: Install system dependencies

   626→            libssl-dev \

   628→            libfuse3-dev \

   632→      - name: Install Rust toolchain
   633→        run: rustup default stable
   634→
   635→      - uses: actions/cache@v4
   636→        with:
   637→          path: |
   638→            ~/.cargo/registry
   639→            ~/.cargo/git
   640→            apps/desktop/src-tauri/target
   641→          key: linux-cargo-${{ hashFiles('apps/desktop/src-tauri/Cargo.lock') }}
   642→          restore-keys: linux-cargo-
   643→
   644→      - name: Cargo check
   645→        working-directory: apps/desktop/src-tauri
   646→        run: cargo check --no-default-features --features fuse
   647→
   648→      - name: Install cargo-llvm-cov
   649→        run: |
   650→          rustup component add llvm-tools-preview
   651→          cargo install cargo-llvm-cov --locked
   652→
   653→      - name: Cargo test with coverage
   654→        run: >-
   655→          cargo llvm-cov
   656→          --manifest-path apps/desktop/src-tauri/Cargo.toml
   657→          --no-default-features --features fuse
   658→          --lcov --output-path desktop-lcov.info
   659→
   660→      - name: Upload desktop coverage to Codecov
   661→        uses: codecov/codecov-action@v5
   662→        with:
   663→          token: ${{ secrets.CODECOV_TOKEN }}
   664→          files: desktop-lcov.info
   665→          flags: desktop
   666→          fail_ci_if_error: false
   667→

```
     1→name: Release Gate
     2→
     3→on:
     4→  pull_request:
     5→    branches: [main]
     6→    types: [opened, synchronize, reopened]
     7→
     8→jobs:
     9→  detect-changes:
    10→    name: Detect Changes Since Last Release
    11→    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    12→    outputs:
    13→      desktop: ${{ steps.check.outputs.desktop || steps.release-check.outputs.desktop }}
    14→      prev_tag: ${{ steps.check.outputs.prev_tag || steps.release-check.outputs.prev_tag }}
    15→      is_release: ${{ steps.release-check.outputs.is_release }}
    16→    steps:
    17→      - name: Check if release PR
    18→        id: release-check
    19→        env:
    20→          HEAD_REF: ${{ github.head_ref }}
    21→        run: |
    22→          if [[ "$HEAD_REF" == release-please--* ]]; then
    23→            echo "is_release=true" >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"
    24→          else
    25→            echo "is_release=false" >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"
    26→            echo "desktop=false" >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"
    27→            echo "prev_tag=" >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"
    28→            echo "Not a release PR — skipping change detection"
    29→          fi
    30→
    31→      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
    32→        if: steps.release-check.outputs.is_release == 'true'
    33→        with:
    34→          fetch-depth: 0
    35→
    36→      - name: Check for desktop-related changes since last release
    37→        if: steps.release-check.outputs.is_release == 'true'
    38→        id: check
    39→        run: |
    40→          # Find the latest non-staging release tag (cipher-box-v* format)
    41→          PREV_TAG=$(git tag --list 'cipher-box-v*' --sort=-v:refname \
    42→            | grep -v 'staging' \
    43→            | head -1)
    44→          # Fallback to legacy v* tags if no cipher-box-v* tags exist
    45→          if [ -z "$PREV_TAG" ]; then
    46→            PREV_TAG=$(git tag --list 'v*' --sort=-v:refname \
    47→              | grep -v 'staging' \
    48→              | grep -v '@' \
    49→              | head -1)
    50→          fi
    51→
    52→          if [ -z "$PREV_TAG" ]; then
    53→            echo "No previous release tag found — assuming desktop changed"
    54→            echo "desktop=true" >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"
    55→            echo "prev_tag=" >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"
    56→            exit 0
    57→          fi
    58→
    59→          echo "Previous release tag: ${PREV_TAG}"
    60→          echo "prev_tag=${PREV_TAG}" >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"
    61→          echo "Diffing ${PREV_TAG}..HEAD"
    62→
    63→          # Desktop-related path patterns (mirrored from desktop-e2e.yml)
    64→          DESKTOP_PATTERNS=(
    65→            'apps/desktop/src/'
    66→            'apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/'
    67→            'apps/desktop/src-tauri/vendor/'
    68→            'apps/desktop/src-tauri/capabilities/'
    69→            'apps/desktop/src-tauri/resources/'
    70→            'apps/desktop/src-tauri/Cargo.toml'
    71→            'apps/desktop/src-tauri/Cargo.lock'
    72→            'apps/desktop/src-tauri/build.rs'
    73→            'apps/desktop/src-tauri/rust-toolchain.toml'
    74→            'apps/desktop/index.html'
    75→            'apps/desktop/vite.config.'
    76→            'apps/desktop/tsconfig'
    77→            'packages/crypto/src/'
    78→            'packages/crypto/tsconfig'
    79→            '.github/workflows/desktop-e2e.yml'
    80→          )
    81→
    82→          CHANGED_FILES=$(git diff --name-only "${PREV_TAG}..HEAD")
    83→          DESKTOP_CHANGED=false
    84→
    85→          for pattern in "${DESKTOP_PATTERNS[@]}"; do
    86→            if echo "$CHANGED_FILES" | grep -Fq "$pattern"; then
    87→              echo "Desktop change detected matching pattern: ${pattern}"
    88→              DESKTOP_CHANGED=true
    89→              break
    90→            fi
    91→          done
    92→
    93→          echo "desktop=${DESKTOP_CHANGED}" >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"
    94→          echo "Desktop changes detected: ${DESKTOP_CHANGED}"
    95→
    96→  verify-e2e:
    97→    name: Verify E2E Passed
    98→    needs: detect-changes
    99→    if: ${{ always() && !cancelled() }}
   100→    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
   101→    timeout-minutes: 40
   102→    steps:
   103→      - name: Fail if detect-changes did not complete (release PR)
   104→        if: startsWith(github.head_ref, 'release-please--') && needs.detect-changes.result != 'success'
   105→        run: |
   106→          echo "::error::detect-changes must succeed before E2E verification."
   107→          exit 1
   108→
   109→      - name: Skip (not a release PR)
   110→        if: "!startsWith(github.head_ref, 'release-please--')"
   111→        run: echo "Not a release PR — E2E gate not applicable"
   112→
   113→      - name: Wait for Web E2E and verify it passed
   114→        if: startsWith(github.head_ref, 'release-please--')
   115→        env:
   116→          GH_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN }}
   117→        run: |
   118→          # Get the latest commit on main (the base the release PR targets)
   119→          MAIN_SHA=$(gh api repos/${{ github.repository }}/git/ref/heads/main --jq '.object.sha')
   120→          echo "Latest main commit: ${MAIN_SHA}"
   121→
   122→          # Poll for the Web E2E run to complete (it triggers on the same push)
   123→          MAX_ATTEMPTS=30
   124→          POLL_INTERVAL=30
   125→          echo "Waiting for Web E2E run to complete (polling every ${POLL_INTERVAL}s, max ${MAX_ATTEMPTS} attempts)..."
   126→
   127→          for attempt in $(seq 1 $MAX_ATTEMPTS); do
   128→            E2E_CONCLUSION=$(gh run list --repo ${{ github.repository }} \
   129→              --workflow=e2e.yml --branch=main --limit=10 \
   130→              --json headSha,conclusion,status \
   131→              --jq "[.[] | select(.headSha == \"${MAIN_SHA}\")][0].conclusion // empty")
   132→
   133→            if [ -n "$E2E_CONCLUSION" ]; then
   134→              echo "Web E2E completed with conclusion: ${E2E_CONCLUSION}"
   135→              break
   136→            fi
   137→
   138→            if [ "$attempt" -eq "$MAX_ATTEMPTS" ]; then
   139→              echo "::error::Web E2E run did not complete within $(( MAX_ATTEMPTS * POLL_INTERVAL / 60 )) minutes for main (${MAIN_SHA})."
   140→              exit 1
   141→            fi
   142→
   143→            echo "Attempt ${attempt}/${MAX_ATTEMPTS}: E2E still running, waiting ${POLL_INTERVAL}s..."
   144→            sleep $POLL_INTERVAL
   145→          done
   146→
   147→          if [ "$E2E_CONCLUSION" != "success" ]; then
   148→            echo "::error::E2E Tests did not pass on main (conclusion: ${E2E_CONCLUSION})"
   149→            exit 1
   150→          fi
   151→          echo "Web E2E: passed"
   152→
   153→      - name: Verify Desktop E2E (if desktop changed)
   154→        if: startsWith(github.head_ref, 'release-please--') && needs.detect-changes.outputs.desktop == 'true'
   155→        env:
   156→          GH_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN }}
   157→          PREV_TAG: ${{ needs.detect-changes.outputs.prev_tag }}
   158→        run: |
   159→          MAIN_SHA=$(gh api repos/${{ github.repository }}/git/ref/heads/main --jq '.object.sha')
   160→          echo "Desktop changes detected since ${PREV_TAG} — verifying Desktop E2E"
   161→
   162→          # Helper: check if a workflow run actually executed desktop-e2e jobs
   163→          # (not skipped due to change detection). Returns 0 if tests ran, 1 if skipped, 2 on API error.
   164→          run_executed_tests() {
   165→            local run_id=$1
   166→            local executed
   167→            if ! executed=$(gh api "repos/${{ github.repository }}/actions/runs/${run_id}/jobs" \
   168→              --jq '[.jobs[] | select(.name | startswith("Desktop E2E")) | select(.conclusion == "success")] | length'); then
   169→              echo "::error::Failed to query jobs for run ${run_id} (API error or rate limit)"
   170→              return 2
   171→            fi
   172→            [ "${executed:-0}" -gt 0 ]
   173→          }
   174→
   175→          # First check if there's a desktop E2E run for HEAD (may still be running)
   176→          HEAD_RUN=$(gh run list --repo ${{ github.repository }} \
   177→            --workflow=desktop-e2e.yml --branch=main --limit=50 \
   178→            --json headSha,conclusion,status,databaseId \
   179→            --jq "[.[] | select(.headSha == \"${MAIN_SHA}\")][0]")
   180→
   181→          HEAD_STATUS=$(echo "$HEAD_RUN" | jq -r '.status // empty')
   182→
   183→          if [ -n "$HEAD_STATUS" ]; then
   184→            HEAD_RUN_ID=$(echo "$HEAD_RUN" | jq -r '.databaseId')
   185→            echo "Desktop E2E run exists for HEAD (run ${HEAD_RUN_ID}) — polling until complete..."
   186→            MAX_ATTEMPTS=40
   187→            POLL_INTERVAL=30
   188→
   189→            for attempt in $(seq 1 $MAX_ATTEMPTS); do
   190→              CONCLUSION=$(gh run view "$HEAD_RUN_ID" --repo ${{ github.repository }} \
   191→                --json conclusion \
   192→                --jq '.conclusion // empty')
   193→
   194→              if [ -n "$CONCLUSION" ]; then
   195→                if [ "$CONCLUSION" != "success" ]; then
   196→                  echo "::error::Desktop E2E Tests did not pass on main (conclusion: ${CONCLUSION})"
   197→                  exit 1
   198→                fi
   199→                # Verify the desktop-e2e jobs actually ran (not skipped)
   200→                run_executed_tests "$HEAD_RUN_ID" && rc=0 || rc=$?
   201→                if [ "$rc" -eq 2 ]; then
   202→                  exit 1
   203→                elif [ "$rc" -eq 0 ]; then
   204→                  echo "Desktop E2E: passed (at HEAD, tests executed)"
   205→                  exit 0
   206→                else
   207→                  echo "Desktop E2E run at HEAD succeeded but tests were skipped (no desktop changes detected by workflow)."
   208→                  echo "Falling through to search for a run that actually executed tests..."
   209→                  break
   210→                fi
   211→              fi
   212→
   213→              if [ "$attempt" -eq "$MAX_ATTEMPTS" ]; then
   214→                echo "::error::Desktop E2E run did not complete within $(( MAX_ATTEMPTS * POLL_INTERVAL / 60 )) minutes."
   215→                exit 1
   216→              fi
   217→
   218→              echo "Attempt ${attempt}/${MAX_ATTEMPTS}: Desktop E2E still running, waiting ${POLL_INTERVAL}s..."
   219→              sleep $POLL_INTERVAL
   220→            done
   221→          fi
   222→
   223→          # No desktop E2E run for HEAD (or HEAD run skipped tests) — find the
   224→          # most recent run in the release range that actually executed tests
   225→          echo "Searching for a Desktop E2E run that executed tests since ${PREV_TAG}..."
   226→
   227→          if [ -z "$PREV_TAG" ]; then
   228→            echo "::error::No previous release tag and no Desktop E2E run for HEAD. Cannot verify desktop changes."
   229→            exit 1
   230→          fi
   231→
   232→          # Get commit SHAs in the release range
   233→          RANGE_COMMITS=$(gh api "repos/${{ github.repository }}/compare/${PREV_TAG}...${MAIN_SHA}" \
   234→            --jq '[.commits[].sha] | join("\n")' 2>/dev/null || echo "")
   235→
   236→          if [ -z "$RANGE_COMMITS" ]; then
   237→            echo "::error::Could not determine commits in range ${PREV_TAG}..${MAIN_SHA}"
   238→            exit 1
   239→          fi
   240→
   241→          # Get recent completed desktop E2E runs (newest first) and find the
   242→          # most recent one whose commit is within the release range AND whose
   243→          # desktop-e2e jobs actually ran (not skipped)
   244→          RUNS=$(gh run list --repo ${{ github.repository }} \
   245→            --workflow=desktop-e2e.yml --branch=main --limit=50 \
   246→            --json headSha,conclusion,status,databaseId \
   247→            --jq '.[] | select(.status == "completed") | "\(.headSha) \(.conclusion) \(.databaseId)"')
   248→
   249→          LATEST_IN_RANGE_SHA=""
   250→          LATEST_IN_RANGE_CONCLUSION=""
   251→
   252→          while IFS=" " read -r sha conclusion run_id; do
   253→            if [ -z "$sha" ]; then
   254→              continue
   255→            fi
   256→            if echo "$RANGE_COMMITS" | grep -Fq "$sha"; then
   257→              if [ "$conclusion" != "success" ]; then
   258→                # Found a failed run in range — report it immediately
   259→                LATEST_IN_RANGE_SHA="$sha"
   260→                LATEST_IN_RANGE_CONCLUSION="$conclusion"
   261→                break
   262→              fi
   263→              # Success run — verify tests actually executed
   264→              run_executed_tests "$run_id" && rc=0 || rc=$?
   265→              if [ "$rc" -eq 2 ]; then
   266→                exit 1
   267→              elif [ "$rc" -eq 0 ]; then
   268→                LATEST_IN_RANGE_SHA="$sha"
   269→                LATEST_IN_RANGE_CONCLUSION="$conclusion"
   270→                LATEST_IN_RANGE_RUN_ID="$run_id"
   271→                break
   272→              else
   273→                echo "Skipping run ${run_id} at ${sha} — tests were skipped (no desktop changes detected by workflow)"
   274→              fi
   275→            fi
   276→          done <<EOF
   277→          $RUNS
   278→          EOF
   279→
   280→          if [ -z "$LATEST_IN_RANGE_SHA" ]; then
   281→            echo "::error::No Desktop E2E run found (with tests actually executed) for any commit in ${PREV_TAG}..HEAD. Desktop code changed but was never verified."
   282→            exit 1
   283→          fi
   284→
   285→          if [ "$LATEST_IN_RANGE_CONCLUSION" != "success" ]; then
   286→            echo "::error::Most recent Desktop E2E run in range (at ${LATEST_IN_RANGE_SHA}) did not succeed (conclusion: ${LATEST_IN_RANGE_CONCLUSION})."
   287→            exit 1
   288→          fi
   289→
   290→          echo "Desktop E2E: passed (run ${LATEST_IN_RANGE_RUN_ID} at ${LATEST_IN_RANGE_SHA} with tests executed)"
   291→
   292→      - name: Skip Desktop E2E (no desktop changes)
   293→        if: startsWith(github.head_ref, 'release-please--') && needs.detect-changes.outputs.desktop != 'true'
   294→        run: echo "No desktop-related changes since last release — skipping Desktop E2E gate"
   295→
   296→      - name: Summary
   297→        if: startsWith(github.head_ref, 'release-please--')
   298→        run: echo "All E2E gates passed — release is safe to merge."
   299→
```

> DEVELOPER

where does that `Deploying to staging` label come from? there should not be any deployment going on during the load test AFAIK

> AGENT

That's from the `environment: staging` line I added to the workflow fix. When a GitHub Actions job references an environment, the UI displays it as "Deploying to staging" — it's not actually deploying anything, it's just how GitHub labels jobs that use environment-scoped secrets.

It's purely cosmetic but admittedly misleading. If it bothers you, the alternative is to move `STAGING_TEST_LOGIN_SECRET`, `STAGING_THROTTLE_BYPASS_SECRET`, and `STAGING_API_URL` from the `staging` environment to repository-level secrets/variables instead. Then we can drop the `environment:` field entirely. Want me to do that, or is this fine since it's just a label?

> DEVELOPER

its perfectly fine for just a label

> AGENT

Got it, no changes needed then. I'll report back when the runs finish.

> AGENT

OK so the 10-client run passed (all 5 tests, 16 min total), but there's a filtering bug — `pnpm test:single -- mixed-workload` passes `-- mixed-workload` to vitest, and CAC puts args after `--` into a separate bucket, not the file filter list. So **all 5 scenarios** ran instead of just `mixed-workload`.

This means the 20 and 30 client runs will also run all scenarios and take a while. I'm still waiting on those two.

In the meantime, here are the 10-client mixed-workload results:

## Mixed Workload — Staging Baselines (2026-03-23)

### Throughput

| Clients | Total Ops | Errors | Throughput | Duration | Data |
|---------|-----------|--------|------------|----------|------|
| 10 | 434 | 0 | 4.49 ops/s | 96.6s | 5.2MB |
| 20 | 856 | 0 | 8.42 ops/s | 101.7s | 9.6MB |
| 30 | 1,299 | 0 | 12.14 ops/s | 107.0s | 14.4MB |

### Latency (p50 / p95 / p99)

| Operation | 10 clients | 20 clients | 30 clients |
|-----------|-----------|-----------|-----------|
| createFolder | 1.9 / 3.1 / 4.4s | 2.0 / 3.3 / 3.6s | 2.4 / 3.5 / 4.2s |
| uploadFile | 2.6 / 3.9 / 4.7s | 2.8 / 4.4 / 4.8s | 3.0 / 4.5 / 5.2s |
| moveItem | 1.9 / 2.6 / 3.4s | 1.9 / 2.9 / 3.4s | 2.3 / 3.1 / 3.7s |
| deleteItem | 815ms / 1.8 / 2.4s | 881ms / 1.8 / 2.0s | 929ms / 1.8 / 2.1s |
| renameItem | 712ms / 1.5 / 1.6s | 758ms / 1.8 / 2.3s | 1.1 / 1.8 / 2.1s |

**Zero errors across all runs.** Throughput scales near-linearly (2.7x at 3x clients), and latency increases are modest — p50 grows ~15-25% from 10→30 clients. The staging API is handling 30 concurrent clients comfortably without saturation.

     1→# Performance Baselines - Phase 18
     2→
     3→## Capture Information
     4→
     5→| Field            | Value                                                           |
     6→| ---------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------- |
     7→| **Capture Date** | 2026-03-07T16:55:31Z (updated 2026-03-08)                       |
     8→| **Environment**  | Staging (api-staging.cipherbox.cc)                              |
     9→| **Kubo Version** | v0.34.0                                                         |
    10→| **API Image**    | v0.24.2-staging-rc-1                                            |
    11→| **VPS**          | Hostinger 76.13.151.200, 4 vCPU, 8GB RAM                        |
    12→| **Script**       | `scripts/baseline-benchmark.sh` + `tests/e2e/load-test.spec.ts` |
    13→| **Iterations**   | 20 measured + 3 warmup per operation (benchmark script)         |
    14→| **File Size**    | 10KB random data (upload/download) for benchmark script         |
    15→
    16→## Methodology
    17→
    18→Baselines are captured using `scripts/baseline-benchmark.sh` which:
    19→
    20→1. Discovers the user's root IPNS name via `GET /vault`
    21→2. Runs 3 warmup iterations (discarded) followed by 20 measured iterations
    22→3. Measures client-side round-trip time via `curl -w "%{time_total}"`
    23→4. Computes p50/p95/p99 from sorted timing values
    24→5. IPNS Publish is excluded (requires signed record) -- captured from Prometheus
    25→
    26→Server-side histograms (`cipherbox_ipfs_ipns_duration_seconds`) provide internal timing without network overhead. Client-side timings from this script include network latency and are useful for end-to-end comparison.
    27→
    28→## Client-Side Timings (curl round-trip)
    29→
    30→| Operation           | p50    | p95    | p99    | Notes                                   |
    31→| ------------------- | ------ | ------ | ------ | --------------------------------------- |
    32→| IPNS Resolve        | 0.147s | 0.224s | 0.278s | `GET /ipns/resolve?ipnsName=<name>`     |
    33→| IPNS Publish        | --     | --     | --     | See Prometheus (requires signed record) |
    34→| IPFS Pin (upload)   | 0.138s | 0.218s | 0.227s | `POST /ipfs/upload` with 10KB file      |
    35→| IPFS Cat (download) | 0.133s | 0.215s | 0.219s | `GET /ipfs/<cid>` for 10KB file         |
    36→
    37→## Server-Side Histograms (Prometheus)
    38→
    39→Captured from the API's `/metrics` endpoint via SSH on 2026-03-08. Includes cumulative data from the baseline-benchmark script run (single-client, ~38 ops) plus the 5-client load test (~377 ops across 5 concurrent users).
    40→
    41→### `cipherbox_ipfs_ipns_duration_seconds` — per-operation breakdown
    42→
    43→Percentiles computed from Prometheus histogram buckets (linear interpolation within bucket boundaries).
    44→
    45→| Operation        | Source  | Count | p50    | p95    | p99    | Mean   | Notes                                         |
    46→| ---------------- | ------- | ----- | ------ | ------ | ------ | ------ | --------------------------------------------- |
    47→| **publish**      | --      | 1367  | 180 ms | 519 ms | 904 ms | 196 ms | Kubo IPNS publish — dominant bottleneck       |
    48→| **resolve**      | network | 529   | 135 ms | 284 ms | 488 ms | 126 ms | Kubo DHT lookup (cache miss)                  |
    49→| **resolve**      | db      | 84    | 35 ms  | 93 ms  | 187 ms | 36 ms  | DB cache hit (success path)                   |
    50→| **resolve** (fb) | db      | 239   | 23 ms  | 84 ms  | 230 ms | 32 ms  | DB cache fallback (Kubo resolve failed)       |
    51→| **resolve**      | network | 42    | 231 ms | 650 ms | 930 ms | 251 ms | Network errors (Kubo resolve timeout/failure) |
    52→| **pin**          | --      | 1923  | 8 ms   | 18 ms  | 31 ms  | 8 ms   | Kubo `pin add` — very fast for small files    |
    53→| **cat**          | --      | 704   | 2 ms   | 5 ms   | 9 ms   | 2 ms   | Kubo `cat` — sub-5ms for cached content       |
    54→
    55→Note: Resolve has 4 series because the API tries Kubo first (source=network), then falls back to DB cache (source=db). The `result` label distinguishes success/error at the Kubo level.
    56→
    57→### Other histograms
    58→
    59→| Metric                                       | Status  | Notes                                      |
    60→| -------------------------------------------- | ------- | ------------------------------------------ |
    61→| `cipherbox_republish_batch_duration_seconds` | No data | Mock TEE provider doesn't report durations |
    62→| `cipherbox_http_request_duration_seconds`    | Below   | Per-route breakdown in HTTP table          |
    63→
    64→## HTTP API Performance (from Prometheus)
    65→
    66→Response times by route — computed from `cipherbox_http_request_duration_seconds` histogram (routes with ≥10 requests):
    67→
    68→| Route                            | Count | p50    | p95    | p99    | Mean   | Notes                              |
    69→| -------------------------------- | ----- | ------ | ------ | ------ | ------ | ---------------------------------- |
    70→| `POST /ipfs/upload` [201]        | 1923  | 8 ms   | 45 ms  | 50 ms  | 13 ms  | Small–medium files (up to 500KB)   |
    71→| `GET /ipfs/:cid` [200]           | 704   | 5 ms   | 10 ms  | 10 ms  | 2 ms   | Extremely fast server-side         |
    72→| `GET /ipns/resolve` [200]        | 852   | 50 ms  | 245 ms | 467 ms | 91 ms  | Includes DB cache fallback path    |
    73→| `GET /ipns/resolve` [404]        | 42    | 231 ms | 650 ms | 930 ms | 251 ms | IPNS name not found (new accounts) |
    74→| `POST /ipns/publish` [201]       | 621   | 165 ms | 477 ms | 871 ms | 172 ms | Kubo IPNS publish — dominant cost  |
    75→| `POST /ipns/publish-batch` [201] | 373   | 275 ms | 793 ms | 959 ms | 303 ms | TEE republish batch path           |
    76→| `GET /vault` [200]               | 12    | 5 ms   | 9 ms   | 10 ms  | 3 ms   |                                    |
    77→| `GET /vault/quota` [200]         | 838   | 5 ms   | 9 ms   | 10 ms  | 1 ms   |                                    |
    78→| `GET /health` [200]              | 539   | 5 ms   | 10 ms  | 32 ms  | 4 ms   |                                    |
    79→| `POST /auth/login` [200]         | 14    | 155 ms | 240 ms | 248 ms | 140 ms | JWT generation + Web3Auth verify   |
    80→| `POST /auth/test-login` [200]    | 11    | 89 ms  | 229 ms | 246 ms | 104 ms |                                    |
    81→| `POST /vault/init` [201]         | 12    | 5 ms   | 9 ms   | 10 ms  | 7 ms   |                                    |
    82→
    83→## Kubo Health Observations
    84→
    85→| Metric             | Value    | Notes                                                             |
    86→| ------------------ | -------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------- |
    87→| Peer Connections   | N/A      | Kubo v0.34.0 does not expose libp2p metrics to Prometheus         |
    88→| Inbound Bandwidth  | N/A      | Grafana panel shows "No data"                                     |
    89→| Outbound Bandwidth | N/A      | Grafana panel shows "No data"                                     |
    90→| Memory Usage       | N/A      | Grafana panel shows "No data"                                     |
    91→| Goroutines         | N/A      | Not exposed by Kubo                                               |
    92→| Datastore Size     | ~320 MiB | From dashboard "Total Storage Used" stat (includes all user data) |
    93→
    94→## Load Test Details
    95→
    96→### Run 1: Manual stress test (single client)
    97→
    98→To populate initial IPNS publish histogram data, a manual stress test was performed via Playwright against the staging web app:
    99→
   100→| Operation     | Count      | Details                                                   |
   101→| ------------- | ---------- | --------------------------------------------------------- |
   102→| Folder create | 8          | stress-01 through -05, nested-subfolder, rapid-fire-01–03 |
   103→| File upload   | 13         | 10 small (7–48 KB) + 3 large (1 MB, 5 MB, 10 MB)          |
   104→| Rename        | 4          | 2 files, 1 folder, 1 large file                           |
   105→| Move          | 3          | Files into stress-02, stress-03, renamed-folder-01        |
   106→| Delete        | 10         | 5 files + 5 folders (some with contents)                  |
   107→| **Total**     | **~38–43** | Each mutates metadata → IPNS publish                      |
   108→
   109→Concurrently, `baseline-benchmark.sh` ran 20 iterations of resolve/pin/cat (+ warmups).
   110→
   111→### Run 2: Automated load test (5 concurrent clients)
   112→
   113→`tests/e2e/load-test.spec.ts` — 5 clients with unique wallets, launched simultaneously (no staggering), each running ~70 file operations.
   114→
   115→| Metric              | Value                                       |
   116→| ------------------- | ------------------------------------------- |
   117→| **Clients**         | 5 (all started at the same time)            |
   118→| **Total ops**       | 395 attempted, 377 succeeded (95.4%)        |
   119→| **Failed ops**      | 18 (all timeouts — 30s per-op limit)        |
   120→| **Total time**      | 317s (5.3 minutes)                          |
   121→| **Throughput**      | 1.25 ops/sec (aggregate across all clients) |
   122→| **IPNS publishes**  | ~377 (one per successful mutation)          |
   123→| **Account cleanup** | 5/5 accounts deleted                        |
   124→
   125→Per-client breakdown:
   126→
   127→| Client | Succeeded | Failed | Notes                                         |
   128→| ------ | --------- | ------ | --------------------------------------------- |
   129→| C1     | 79        | 0      | Finished first — got head start before others |
   130→| C2     | 72        | 7      | Warm-up timeout + move dialog timeouts        |
   131→| C3     | 78        | 1      | Single upload timeout in images folder        |
   132→| C4     | 73        | 6      | Warm-up timeout + folder creation timeout     |
   133→| C5     | 75        | 4      | Warm-up timeout + batch delete timeout        |
   134→
   135→Failure pattern: C2–C5 all timed out on their warm-up folder operation (30s) while C1 raced ahead. Subsequent failures clustered around move dialog waits and folder navigation — consistent with server-side IPNS publish latency increasing under concurrent load. The 5-client workload drove ~1,300 IPNS publishes through Kubo simultaneously.
   136→
   137→## Notes
   138→
   139→- Client-side timings captured 2026-03-07 via `baseline-benchmark.sh` (5 runs × 20 iterations per operation = 100 data points per operation, 300 total across resolve/pin/cat)
   140→- Server-side histogram values captured 2026-03-08 via SSH to staging VPS (`curl http://localhost:3000/metrics`) — cumulative across benchmark script + 5-client load test
   141→- 5-client load test run 2026-03-08 via `tests/e2e/load-test.spec.ts` — 5 concurrent Playwright browsers, ~70 ops each, all launched simultaneously
   142→- IPNS Publish is the dominant bottleneck at ~519ms p95 server-side (904ms p99) — essentially all time in Kubo, negligible HTTP overhead
   143→- Under 5-client concurrency, publish-batch p95 reaches 793ms (vs 477ms for single publish) — batching multiple IPNS names increases latency
   144→- IPNS Resolve network errors (n=42) have high latency (p50=231ms, p95=650ms) — these are Kubo timeouts that trigger DB cache fallback
   145→- DB cache fallback is fast (p50=23ms) and handles 74% of resolve calls (239/323 db-path resolves were error-fallback)
   146→- Pin and Cat remain extremely fast under load (p50=8ms and 2ms) — client-side timings (~130ms) are dominated by network latency
   147→- Kubo v0.34.0 does not expose libp2p metrics — Kubo Health section is N/A
   148→- TEE Republish Batch Duration shows "No data" — mock TEE provider doesn't report real durations
   149→- These baselines will be compared against post-Phase 19 and Phase 22 measurements
   150→- The benchmark script is deterministic: same iterations, same file size, same warmup count
   151→- The load test script (`tests/e2e/load-test.spec.ts`) is parameterized: `LOAD_TEST_CLIENTS` env var (default: 5)
   152→
   153→## Comparison Target
   154→
   155→Phase 22 (after IPFS infrastructure changes) will re-run this benchmark and document:
   156→
   157→- Performance regression threshold: >20% p95 increase requires investigation
   158→- Performance improvement targets vary by operation (see Phase 22 plan)
   159→

     1→# Performance Baselines - Phase 19 (Someguy IPNS Sidecar)
     2→
     3→## Capture Information
     4→
     5→| Field            | Value                                                              |
     6→| ---------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------ |
     7→| **Capture Date** | 2026-03-23                                                         |
     8→| **Environment**  | Staging (api-staging.cipherbox.cc) + Local (192.168.133.114)       |
     9→| **Kubo Version** | v0.40.0                                                            |
    10→| **Someguy**      | v0.11.1 (ghcr.io/ipfs/someguy)                                     |
    11→| **API Image**    | cipher-box-v0.26.5                                                 |
    12→| **VPS**          | Hostinger KVM2, 4 vCPU, 8GB RAM                                    |
    13→| **Test Suite**   | SDK E2E (83 tests) + Load tests (vitest, custom metrics collector) |
    14→| **DHT Warm-up**  | ~8 hours on staging, ~10 minutes on local                          |
    15→
    16→## Someguy Configuration
    17→
    18→| Setting                     | Previous (broken, PR #284) | Fixed (PR #325)              |
    19→| --------------------------- | -------------------------- | ---------------------------- |
    20→| libp2p port 4004            | Not exposed                | Exposed (TCP + UDP)          |
    21→| DHT mode                    | `standard`                 | `accelerated`                |
    22→| Connection limits           | 50/300                     | 100/3000 (defaults)          |
    23→| `SOMEGUY_LIBP2P_MAX_MEMORY` | 512MB                      | 1073741824 (1GB)             |
    24→| Container memory            | 768MB                      | 2GB                          |
    25→| Container CPU               | 0.5                        | 1.0                          |
    26→| Healthcheck start period    | 30s                        | 60s                          |
    27→| Fallback URL                | None                       | `https://delegated-ipfs.dev` |
    28→
    29→Root cause of Phase 19's initial failure: no libp2p port exposed meant DHT couldn't receive inbound connections, `standard` mode skipped accelerated FullRT client, and tight resource limits triggered libp2p Resource Manager errors.
    30→
    31→## SDK E2E Results (83 tests)
    32→
    33→| Environment       | Passed | Failed | Skipped |
    34→| ----------------- | ------ | ------ | ------- |
    35→| Local (someguy)   | 83     | 0      | 0       |
    36→| Staging (someguy) | 83     | 0      | 0       |
    37→
    38→All 83 tests pass with zero errors on both environments.
    39→
    40→## Load Test: IPNS Publish Storm (5 clients × 50 cycles = 750 ops)
    41→
    42→### Cross-environment comparison
    43→
    44→| Metric           | Staging (someguy, warm DHT) | Local (delegated-ipfs.dev) | Local (someguy, cold DHT) |
    45→| ---------------- | --------------------------- | -------------------------- | ------------------------- |
    46→| Duration         | **49.1s**                   | 69.7s                      | 89.3s                     |
    47→| Throughput       | **15.28 ops/s**             | 10.75 ops/s                | 8.39 ops/s                |
    48→| Errors           | **0**                       | **0**                      | **0**                     |
    49→| createFolder p50 | **468ms**                   | 606ms                      | 728ms                     |
    50→| createFolder p95 | **848ms**                   | 1.22s                      | 1.07s                     |
    51→| createFolder p99 | **1.0s**                    | 1.62s                      | 1.27s                     |
    52→| deleteItem p50   | **246ms**                   | 395ms                      | 469ms                     |
    53→| deleteItem p95   | **476ms**                   | 690ms                      | 795ms                     |
    54→| renameItem p50   | **182ms**                   | 305ms                      | 520ms                     |
    55→| renameItem p95   | **377ms**                   | 704ms                      | 1.06s                     |
    56→
    57→### Comparison vs Phase 18 baselines
    58→
    59→| Metric              | Phase 18 (Kubo direct, 5 clients) | Phase 19 staging (someguy, 5 clients) | Change         |
    60→| ------------------- | --------------------------------- | ------------------------------------- | -------------- |
    61→| Throughput          | 1.25 ops/sec                      | **15.28 ops/sec**                     | **+12.2x**     |
    62→| Error rate          | 4.6% (18/395)                     | **0%** (0/750)                        | **Eliminated** |
    63→| Total ops attempted | 395                               | 750                                   | +90% more ops  |
    64→| Total ops succeeded | 377                               | 750                                   | +99%           |
    65→
    66→Note: Phase 18 measured via Playwright browser automation (higher overhead per op), Phase 19 via SDK direct calls. The measurement difference accounts for some of the throughput gap, but the zero error rate is the significant improvement — Phase 18 had 18 timeouts under identical concurrency.
    67→
    68→## Load Test: Mixed Workload (5 clients × 45 mixed ops)
    69→
    70→| Metric           | Staging (someguy) | Local (delegated-ipfs.dev) | Local (someguy, cold DHT) |
    71→| ---------------- | ----------------- | -------------------------- | ------------------------- |
    72→| Duration         | **23.6s**         | 40.4s                      | 58.9s                     |
    73→| Throughput       | **9.32 ops/s**    | 5.50 ops/s                 | 3.68 ops/s                |
    74→| Errors           | **0**             | **0**                      | **0**                     |
    75→| createFolder p50 | **511ms**         | 840ms                      | 1.4s                      |
    76→| deleteItem p50   | **215ms**         | 338ms                      | 539ms                     |
    77→| moveItem p50     | **442ms**         | 659ms                      | 1.2s                      |
    78→| renameItem p50   | **195ms**         | 390ms                      | 623ms                     |
    79→| uploadFile p50   | **613ms**         | 1.1s                       | 1.5s                      |
    80→| Data transferred | 2.5MB             | 2.9MB                      | 2.6MB                     |
    81→
    82→## Key Observations
    83→
    84→1. **DHT warm-up matters significantly**: Staging someguy (8h uptime) is ~2x faster than local someguy (10min uptime). The accelerated DHT routing table fills over time, improving lookup speed.
    85→
    86→2. **Someguy with warm DHT beats delegated-ipfs.dev**: 42% higher throughput and ~40-50% lower median latency across all operations. The public fleet advantage disappears once the local sidecar's DHT is populated.
    87→
    88→3. **Zero errors across all test runs**: Both SDK E2E (83 tests) and load tests (750+ ops) complete with zero failures, compared to Phase 18's 4.6% error rate.
    89→
    90→4. **DB-first resolve architecture unchanged**: IPNS resolution still goes through the database as primary source of truth. Someguy's value is in reliable DHT publishing for TEE republishing and the standalone recovery tool, not in replacing DB resolves.
    91→
    92→5. **Fallback to delegated-ipfs.dev**: Configured via `DELEGATED_ROUTING_FALLBACK_URL`. Prometheus counter `cipherbox_delegated_routing_fallbacks_total` tracks when primary (someguy) fails and fallback is used.
    93→
    94→## Prometheus Metrics Available
    95→
    96→New metrics added in this phase for ongoing monitoring:
    97→
    98→| Metric                                        | Labels                      | Purpose                                       |
    99→| --------------------------------------------- | --------------------------- | --------------------------------------------- |
   100→| `cipherbox_delegated_routing_requests_total`  | operation, backend, outcome | Every routing request tagged primary/fallback |
   101→| `cipherbox_delegated_routing_fallbacks_total` | operation                   | Count of primary→fallback triggers            |
   102→| `cipherbox_ipns_publish_duration_seconds`     | outcome                     | Delegated routing publish latency             |
   103→| `cipherbox_ipns_resolve_duration_seconds`     | source, outcome             | End-to-end resolve latency                    |
   104→
   105→## Comparison Targets
   106→
   107→Phase 22 (Performance Baselines Completion) should:
   108→
   109→- Re-capture server-side Prometheus histograms for direct comparison with Phase 18 internal timings
   110→- Add client-side instrumentation for real user latency measurement
   111→- Run k6 load tests at higher concurrency (10, 20, 50 clients)
   112→- Document capacity limits and scaling recommendations
   113→- Performance regression threshold: >20% p95 increase requires investigation
   114→

Now I have a comprehensive understanding. Let me create the final report with all the details:

## Upload Flow End-to-End Trace - Complete Analysis

I've thoroughly traced the `uploadFile` flow from SDK client through API server. Here's the comprehensive breakdown:

### 1. SDK CLIENT SIDE (packages/sdk-core → packages/sdk)

**Entry Point:** `CipherBoxClient.uploadFile()` in `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/packages/sdk/src/client.ts:572`

**Sequence of operations:**
1. **Load folder state** from internal FolderTree (in-memory)
2. **Call sdkCore.uploadFile()** - the core encryption & upload pipeline
3. **Add FilePointer to folder** (metadata update)
4. **Batch publish IPNS** for file metadata
5. **Update folder metadata and publish** to IPNS
6. **Update internal state** (folder tree, sequence numbers)
7. **Emit SDK events** (`file:uploaded`, `folder:updated`)
8. **Re-wrap keys for shares** (best-effort, logged on failure)

---

### 2. CORE UPLOAD FUNCTION (packages/sdk-core/src/upload/index.ts:64)

**Stateless `uploadFile()` function - performs the atomic encrypt → upload → register cycle:**

**Step 1: Key Generation**
- Generate random AES-256 file key
- Generate random IV

**Step 2: Encryption**
- Encrypt file content with `encryptAesGcm()` (AES-256-GCM)
- Wrap file key with user's public key (ECIES)

**Step 3: Upload to IPFS**
- Call `addToIpfs()` with encrypted ciphertext
- Returns CID + encrypted size

**Step 4: Create File Metadata (per-file IPNS record)**
- Generate random Ed25519 IPNS keypair for the file
- Create FileMetadata object (v1 schema): CID, wrapped key, IV, size, MIME type, timestamps
- Encrypt metadata with parent folder's key
- Upload encrypted metadata to IPFS (returns metadataCid)
- Create IPNS record pointing to `/ipfs/{metadataCid}` with sequence #1
- Marshal + base64 encode the record
- **TEE enrollment:** If TEE keys present, wrap IPNS private key with TEE public key
- Return: file's IPNS name, record payload (for batch publish), ECIES-wrapped IPNS key

**Step 5: Memory cleanup**
- Clear file key from memory on success/failure

---

### 3. API UPLOAD ENDPOINT (apps/api/src/ipfs/ipfs.controller.ts:48)

**`POST /ipfs/upload` - Receives encrypted file blob**

**Handler: `upload()` at line 86**

**Timing instrumentation:**
```typescript
const endPinTimer = this.metricsService.ipfsIpnsDuration.startTimer({
  operation: 'pin',
  source: '',
});
```

**Steps:**
1. **Check quota** - Call `vaultService.checkQuota(userId, fileSize)` → returns boolean
   - If exceeded, throw `PayloadTooLargeException` (413)

2. **Pin to IPFS** - Call `ipfsProvider.pinFile(file.buffer)` (LocalProvider)
   - Timing tracked via histogram timer
   - LocalProvider calls Kubo API: `POST /api/v0/add?pin=true&cid-version=1`
   - Returns: `{cid, size}`
   - On error: record error in histogram, throw

3. **Record quota** - Call `vaultService.recordPin(userId, cid, size)`
   - Creates `PinnedCid` database record
   - If fails, **atomic rollback**: `ipfsProvider.unpinFile(cid)`, throw

4. **Increment metrics** (atomic counter operations)
   - `metricsService.fileUploads.inc()`
   - `metricsService.fileUploadBytes.inc(size)`

5. **Return response** - `UploadResponseDto`: `{cid, size, recorded: true}`

---

### 4. FILE METADATA PUBLICATION (packages/sdk-core/src/file/index.ts:56)

**`createFileMetadata()` - Single per-file IPNS operation**

Already described in Step 4 above. Returns record payload for batch publishing.

---

### 5. IPNS PUBLISH FLOW (Two separate calls after upload)

#### **Call 1: File metadata IPNS batch publish**

From client at `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/packages/sdk/src/client.ts:612`:
```typescript
await sdkCore.batchPublishIpnsRecords([uploadResult.ipnsRecord], this.ctx);
```

Routes to: `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/packages/sdk-core/src/ipns/index.ts:94`

**`batchPublishIpnsRecords()` - Publishes multiple IPNS records**
- Takes array of record payloads: `{ipnsName, recordBase64, metadataCid, encryptedIpnsPrivateKey?, keyEpoch?}`
- Calls generated API client: `ipnsControllerPublishBatch()`
- HTTP endpoint: `POST /ipns/publish-batch`

#### **Call 2: Folder metadata IPNS publish**

From client at `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/packages/sdk/src/client.ts:614`:
```typescript
const { newSequenceNumber } = await sdkCore.updateFolderMetadataAndPublish({
  children: updatedChildren,
  folderKey: folder.folderKey,
  ipnsPrivateKey: folder.ipnsKeypair.privateKey,
  ipnsName: folderIpnsName,
  sequenceNumber: folder.sequenceNumber,
  ctx: this.ctx,
});
```

Routes to: `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/packages/sdk-core/src/folder/index.ts:159`

**`updateFolderMetadataAndPublish()` sequence:**
1. Create v2 FolderMetadata (list of children)
2. Encrypt with folder key
3. Upload encrypted metadata to IPFS via `addToIpfs()`
4. Create IPNS record with incremented sequence number
5. Call `createAndPublishIpnsRecord()` - single record publish with conflict retry
6. On 409 conflict (concurrent modification), resolve IPNS to get latest seq and retry once
7. Return: `{cid, newSequenceNumber}`

---

### 6. API IPNS PUBLISH ENDPOINTS

#### **Endpoint 1: `POST /ipns/publish` - Single record**

File: `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/apps/api/src/ipns/ipns.controller.ts:40`

Handler at line 71 calls `ipnsService.publishRecord()`:

**Timing instrumentation:**
```typescript
const endTimer = this.metricsService.ipfsIpnsDuration.startTimer({
  operation: 'publish',
  source: '',
});
```

**Steps in ipns.service.ts:43:**
1. Validate base64-encoded record
2. **Upsert database entry** (FolderIpns):
   - Check if exists
   - Conflict detection: if `expectedSequenceNumber` provided, verify matches current
   - If mismatch: throw 409 ConflictException with currentSequenceNumber
   - Update or create FolderIpns entry with new sequence = current + 1
   - Save to DB (authoritative source for fallback)

3. **Fire-and-forget delegated routing publish:**
   ```typescript
   const publishStart = process.hrtime.bigint();
   this.delegatedRouting.publish(dto.ipnsName, recordBytes)
     .catch(error => { /* log warn */ })
     .then(outcome => {
       const publishElapsed = Number(process.hrtime.bigint() - publishStart) / 1e9;
       this.metricsService.ipnsPublishDuration.observe(
         { outcome: outcome === 'error' ? 'error' : 'success' },
         publishElapsed
       );
     });
   ```

   - **CRITICAL:** Returns immediately after DB save (doesn't wait for DHT propagation)
   - DB record is reliable source; delegated routing is eventually consistent
   - DHT propagation via someguy takes ~10-30s per record
   - Metrics are recorded asynchronously in detached promise

4. **Auto-enroll for TEE republishing** if encrypted key provided
5. **Increment counter** - `metricsService.ipnsPublishes.inc({type: 'single'})`
6. **Return response** - `{success: true, ipnsName, sequenceNumber}`

**Error handling:**
- 400: Invalid base64
- 409: Sequence mismatch
- 502: Delegated routing failures are logged but DB write still succeeds

#### **Endpoint 2: `POST /ipns/publish-batch` - Multiple records**

File: `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/apps/api/src/ipns/ipns.controller.ts:82`

Handler at line 113 calls `ipnsService.publishBatch()`:

**Batch processing (ipns.service.ts:120):**
- CONCURRENCY limit: 10 concurrent records
- Process in chunks of 10, delegating each to `publishRecord()`
- Supports partial success: individual failures don't fail batch
- Exception: 409 conflict on ANY record fails entire batch (global conflict detection)
- Returns: `{results: [], totalSucceeded, totalFailed}`
- Increments counter: `metricsService.ipnsPublishes.inc({type: 'batch'}, totalSucceeded)`

---

### 7. PROMETHEUS METRICS

**File:** `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/apps/api/src/metrics/metrics.service.ts`

#### **Histogram Metrics (Duration measurements):**

| Metric Name | Labels | Buckets | Used For |
|---|---|---|---|
| `cipherbox_ipfs_ipns_duration_seconds` | `operation`, `result`, `source` | 0.001, 0.005, 0.01, 0.025, 0.05, 0.1, 0.25, 0.5, 1, 2.5, 5, 10, 20, 30 | IPFS pin/cat, IPNS publish/resolve |
| `cipherbox_ipns_publish_duration_seconds` | `outcome` | 0.1, 0.25, 0.5, 1, 2, 5, 10, 30, 60 | Delegated routing publish latency (fire-and-forget, async) |
| `cipherbox_ipns_resolve_duration_seconds` | `source`, `outcome` | 0.05, 0.1, 0.25, 0.5, 1, 2, 5, 10, 30 | IPNS resolution with fallback (network vs DB cache) |
| `cipherbox_http_request_duration_seconds` | `method`, `route`, `status_code` | 0.01, 0.05, 0.1, 0.25, 0.5, 1, 2.5, 5, 10 | End-to-end HTTP request (all endpoints) |

**Operation labels for `cipherbox_ipfs_ipns_duration_seconds`:**
- `pin` - IPFS pinning (via `/ipfs/upload` endpoint)
- `cat` - IPFS file retrieval (via `/ipfs/:cid` endpoint)
- `publish` - IPNS publish (both single & batch, tracked at HTTP level)
- `resolve` - IPNS resolution (via `/ipns/resolve` endpoint)

#### **Counter Metrics:**

| Metric | Incremented When |
|---|---|
| `cipherbox_file_uploads_total` | `/ipfs/upload` returns 201 |
| `cipherbox_file_upload_bytes_total` | Incremented by pinned file size |
| `cipherbox_ipns_publishes_total` (label: `type`) | `/ipns/publish` (type=single) or `/ipns/publish-batch` (type=batch), by count |
| `cipherbox_delegated_routing_requests_total` (labels: `operation`, `backend`, `outcome`) | Delegated routing API calls |

#### **Gauge Metrics (DB-polled every 30s):**

- `cipherbox_users_total`
- `cipherbox_files_total` (pinned count from PinnedCid table)
- `cipherbox_storage_bytes_total` (sum of pinned file sizes)
- `cipherbox_ipns_entries_total` (label: `record_type` - folder/file)
- `cipherbox_republish_schedule_total` (label: `status`)

---

### 8. STRUCTURED LOGGING

**File:** `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/apps/api/src/ipns/ipns.service.ts`

Logger calls (NestJS Logger):

| Log Level | Context | Message |
|---|---|---|
| `debug` | Line 323 | IPNS name resolved successfully (from network) |
| `warn` | Line 328 | Delegated routing failed, falling back to DB cache |
| `log` | Line 348-349 | DB cache has newer sequence than network |
| `log` | Line 369 | Resolved from DB cache |
| `error` | Line 407 | Failed to extract CID from IPNS record |
| `error` | Line 427 | Failed to parse IPNS record |

**No structured JSON logging present** - basic text logs via `Logger` service.

**Duration fields manually calculated:**
```typescript
const elapsed = Number(process.hrtime.bigint() - startTime) / 1e9;
this.metricsService.ipnsResolveDuration.observe({ source, outcome: timerResult }, elapsed);
```

---

### 9. KEY LATENCY BOTTLENECKS

**Observable via metrics:**

1. **`cipherbox_ipfs_ipns_duration_seconds{operation="pin"}`** - IPFS pinning latency
   - Includes: Kubo API call, CIDv1 computation, persistence to blockstore
   - Expected: 100-500ms for small files

2. **`cipherbox_http_request_duration_seconds{route="/ipfs/upload"}`** - Full upload endpoint latency
   - Includes: Multipart parsing, quota check (DB query), pinning, quota record (DB insert)
   - Expected: 200-1000ms

3. **`cipherbox_http_request_duration_seconds{route="/ipns/publish*"}`** - IPNS publish endpoint latency
   - Includes: Base64 decode, upsert FolderIpns (DB write), TEE enrollment (best-effort)
   - **Does NOT include** DHT propagation (fire-and-forget)
   - Expected: 10-100ms (DB-bound, not network-bound)

4. **`cipherbox_ipns_publish_duration_seconds`** - Delegated routing latency (async, recorded separately)
   - Includes: HTTP call to delegated routing service
   - Expected: 100-5000ms depending on service availability

5. **`cipherbox_ipns_resolve_duration_seconds{source="network"}`** - IPNS resolution with network fallback
   - Includes: Delegated routing call + DB cache check + signature verification
   - Expected: 100-10000ms (high variance due to delegated routing availability)

---

### 10. SEQUENCE DIAGRAM: SINGLE FILE UPLOAD

```
Client SDK                    API /ipfs/upload            IPFS Kubo          Database
    |                               |                         |                 |
    | 1. encryptFile()              |                         |                 |
    |<--encryption complete-------->|                         |                 |
    |                               |                         |                 |
    | 2. POST /ipfs/upload          |                         |                 |
    |--form data (ciphertext)------->| 3. checkQuota()         |                 |
    |                               |-----query PinnedCid---------->|         |
    |                               |<-----bytes_used (cached)----|         |
    |                               |                         |                 |
    |                               | 4. POST /api/v0/add      |                 |
    |                               |---formData ------------->|                 |
    |                               |<-----cid, size -----------|                 |
    |                               |                         |                 |
    |                               | 5. recordPin()           |                 |
    |                               |-----insert PinnedCid--------|------>|
    |                               |<-----pin recorded---------|         |
    |                               |                         |                 |
    |<---{cid, size, recorded}------|                         |                 |
    |                               |                         |                 |
    | 6. createFileMetadata()        |                         |                 |
    |  - generate IPNS keypair       |                         |                 |
    |  - create IPNS record #1       |                         |                 |
    |                               |                         |                 |
    | 7. POST /ipfs/upload (metadata)|                         |                 |
    |---encrypted metadata---------->| [same as step 2-4]     |                 |
    |<---{metadataCid}------|                         |                 |
    |                               |                         |                 |
    | 8. POST /ipns/publish-batch   |                         |                 |
    |--[file IPNS record]----------->| 9. upsertFolderIpns()   |                 |
    |                               |-----update/insert-------|------>|
    |<---{success}----------|                         |                 |
    |  [async] delegated routing    |                         |                 |
    |                               |                         |                 |
    | 10. updateFolderMetadata()     |                         |                 |
    |  - add FilePointer             |                         |                 |
    |  - encrypt folder metadata     |                         |                 |
    |                               |                         |                 |
    | 11. POST /ipfs/upload (folder) |                         |                 |
    |--encrypted folder metadata---->| [same as step 2-4]     |                 |
    |<---{folderCid}--------|                         |                 |
    |                               |                         |                 |
    | 12. createAndPublishIpnsRecord |                         |                 |
    | (folder, seq = old_seq + 1)   |                         |                 |
    |                               |                         |                 |
    | 13. POST /ipns/publish        |                         |                 |
    |--[folder IPNS record, seq+1]-->| 14. upsertFolderIpns()  |                 |
    |                               |-----update seq---------|------>|
    |                               | [conflict retry if 409] |                 |
    |<---{success, seq}--------|                         |                 |
    |  [async] delegated routing    |                         |                 |
    |                               |                         |                 |
```

> AGENT

Excellent research. Now I have the full picture. Here's what the uploadFile flow actually does (it's **5 API calls**, not 1):

1. **Encrypt file** (client-side, CPU)
2. **POST /ipfs/upload** — pin encrypted ciphertext to IPFS via Kubo
3. **POST /ipfs/upload** — pin encrypted file metadata to IPFS
4. **POST /ipns/publish-batch** — publish file's IPNS record (DB write + async DHT)
5. **POST /ipfs/upload** — pin updated encrypted folder metadata to IPFS
6. **POST /ipns/publish** — publish folder's IPNS record with incremented seq#

So the SDK-measured "uploadFile" latency is the sum of 3 IPFS pins + 2 IPNS publishes + client-side encryption.

The Prometheus metrics I'll query after the runs:
- `cipherbox_ipfs_ipns_duration_seconds{operation="pin"}` — per-pin Kubo latency
- `cipherbox_http_request_duration_seconds{route="/ipfs/upload"}` — full upload endpoint
- `cipherbox_http_request_duration_seconds{route="/ipns/publish"}` — IPNS publish endpoint
- `cipherbox_ipns_publish_duration_seconds` — async delegated routing latency

I'll SSH into the staging VPS and scrape Prometheus after each run completes. Still waiting on the 50/75/100 client runs.

> DEVELOPER

all 3 runs look like they were cancelled :(

> AGENT

That's likely the `timeout-minutes: 30` on the job. Running all 5 scenarios at those client counts would easily exceed 30 minutes — the 10-client run already took 16 minutes for all scenarios.

Two fixes needed:
1. **Increase the timeout** for high-client runs
2. **Fix the scenario filter** so it only runs the selected scenario instead of all 5

The filtering fix is the bigger win — running just mixed-workload at 50 clients should take ~5 minutes, not 30+. Let me fix both.

   191→        if: always()

   193→        with:
   194→          name: load-test-metrics-${{ inputs.scenario }}-${{ inputs.client_count }}clients

   196→          retention-days: 30
   197→          if-no-files-found: ignore
   198→

> AGENT

Good point about not running them concurrently — they all hit the same staging API, so they'd interfere with each other's results. I'll add a concurrency group so only one load test runs at a time, fix the scenario filter, and bump the timeout.

  "new_string": "concurrency:\n  group: load-test-${{ inputs.environment }}\n  cancel-in-progress: false\n\njobs:\n  load-test:\n    name: 'Load Test: ${{ inputs.scenario }} (${{ inputs.client_count }} clients)'\n    runs-on: ubuntu-latest\n    environment: ${{ inputs.environment == 'staging' && 'staging' || '' }}\n    timeout-minutes: 60"
